### E051: Cycle 13: T04 — the E046 "~1.3% below heuristic" is a constant offset, not a slope deficit: mean σ_T(n) − 6.95212 ln n ≈ −1.39 steps from 10⁴ to 10¹⁸; variance slope 101.1 vs random-walk 101.39; the pure random-walk model predicts the opposite-sign offset (≈ +2.0), and the gap comes from the small-number endgame below ~10⁶
- **Date:** 2026-11-08 ~8:09–8:30 AM PST · **Cycle:** 13 · **Threads:** T04
- **Question this cycle:** Is the 1.3–1.5% shortfall of Collatz total stopping times against the random-walk prediction (E046, n ≤ 10⁷) a finite-size effect that fades as n grows, or a real difference in the growth rate?
- **Why this, now:** E046 left it open (H4.1, "Kontorovich–Lagarias finite-N bias", "variance vs RW"). It can be settled by direct computation in one cycle. Self-chosen; no outside suggestion. Choosing an open thread over a new seed is not a rule.

**Direct observations** (what I computed or ran myself this cycle; script path + output):
- `notes/cycle13/collatz_big.c` mode A (exhaustive, uint16 cache, n ≤ 10⁸; 3.2 s): σ_T(27) = 70 (= A006577's 111 C-steps minus 41 odd steps); decade [10⁶,10⁷) mean σ_T = 105.49467, identical to E046's `results.json`. Per-decade mean of dev(n) := σ_T(n) − K ln n, K = 2/ln(4/3) = 6.952119: [10³,10⁴) −0.212; [10⁴,10⁵) −1.296; [10⁵,10⁶) −1.276; [10⁶,10⁷) −1.387; [10⁷,10⁸) −1.409. Output `modeA_1e8.out`.
- Mode B (uniform random n in each decade 10³…10¹⁸, 400 000 per decade per seed, two seeds, unsigned 128-bit arithmetic): dev stays between −1.22 and −1.56 for every decade from 10⁴ to 10¹⁸ (per-decade s.e. 0.05–0.10). Outputs `modeB_s1.out`, `modeB_s2.out`; pooled analysis `analyze.py` → `analysis.json`.
- Weighted fit over decades 10⁴–10¹⁸: dev = −1.350 ± 0.034 + (−0.0016 ± 0.0014)·ln n, so the implied growth coefficient is 6.9505 ± 0.0014 against K = 6.95212 (difference 1.2 s.e.). A constant-only fit gives C = −1.385 ± 0.013. Both fits have χ² ≈ 33–34 on 13–14 d.o.f., so the scatter between decades is about 1.6× what the sampling errors predict (unexplained; see Unknowns).
- Variance: Var(σ_T) per decade vs mean ln n, OLS slope 101.13 per unit ln n, intercept −131.7 (e.g. 4221 at ln n ≈ 42.97, i.e. Var/ln n = 98.2 there).
- `brw.c`, pure biased-random-walk model (steps ln(3/2) or −ln 2 with probability ½, start ln n, stop at the first Z ≤ 0): dev ≈ +1.91 to +2.25 at every size (s.e. 0.05–0.11), mean undershoot below 0 ≈ 0.296–0.302, and K × undershoot ≈ 2.05–2.10. Var/ln n ≈ 104.1 → 101.8 as ln n grows 11 → 43 (fit slope ≈ 101.9, intercept ≈ +24). Output `brw.out`.
- `wald.c`, decomposition for random n ∈ [10¹⁵, 10¹⁶): run the real map until the first iterate y < m, then σ_T(n) = τ_m + σ_T(y). The Wald residual R_m := τ_m − (ln n − ln y)/|μ| is −0.031 ± 0.076 for m = 10⁶, −0.542 ± 0.082 for m = 10⁴, and −0.972 ± 0.085 for m = 10³. For n ∈ [10¹², 10¹³) and m = 10⁴ it is −0.536 ± 0.071, the same as from 10¹⁵. Mean dev(y) at the landing point is −1.494 (m = 10⁶), −0.983 (10⁴), and −0.553 (10³). The summed "+1" correction Σ log(1 + 1/(3x)) above m is 7e-7 to 7e-4 in log units, which is negligible.
- `parity_bands.c` (from 10¹⁵, 400 000 trajectories): odd-step fraction among visits in each decade band is 0.4998–0.5004 for bands 10⁷–10¹⁵, 0.4985–0.4992 for 10⁴–10⁶, 0.5045 at 10³, 0.550 at 10², 0.458 at 10¹, and 0.274 at 10⁰. Visits per decade band ≈ 6.4 × 10⁶ / 400 000 ≈ 16.0, against K ln 10 = 16.008. The naive s.e. (≈0.0002) treats visits as independent, but trajectories merge, so the real uncertainty is larger. Output `parity_bands_d15.out`.

**Retrieved information** (what sources say; quote numbers exactly; [E2/E3/E4] + URL):
- Kontorovich & Lagarias, "Stochastic models for the 3x+1 and 5x+3 problems" ([E2/E3] survey, [arXiv:0910.1944 HTML](https://arxiv.org/html/0910.1944)): in the 3x+1 BRW model the total stopping time is S∞(n) = min{k>0 : Z_k ≤ 0, Z₀ = log n}, and they write "E[S∞(n)] = (1/|μ|) log n ≈ 6.95212 log n". Theorem 3.2 (credited to Borovkov & Pfeifer) normalizes (S∞ − (1/μ) log n)/(μ^{−3/2} σ √log n) → N(0,1) with μ = |½ log(3/4)| and σ = ½ log 3 (here σ is the per-step standard deviation), which implies Var ≈ (log 3)²/4 / μ³ · log n = 101.387 log n. In Definition 2.4's prose, the HTML rendering gives the variance as "½ log 3 (½ log 4/3)^{3/2}". That formula is not consistent with Theorem 3.2: the exponent should be −3/2, and "variance" there is a standard-deviation-type quantity. I take this as a typo or rendering issue. I did not check the PDF.
- "A Gaussian law for Collatz total stopping times" ([E4]: unrefereed Zenodo deposit, [doi:10.5281/zenodo.23046895](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23046895), abstract only): claims a proof that the convergent set has a positive natural density h and that (τ − c log n)/√(v log n) is Gaussian, with c = 2/d, v = 2(log 3)²/d³, d = log(4/3). Its v = 101.387, the same constant. A companion deposit ([doi:10.5281/zenodo.23268828](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23268828), [E4]) claims extremal coefficients up to 7.626913. These would be major results; I have not read the proofs, seen any expert assessment, or found a refereed version, so I treat them only as a restatement of the model constants.
- Chamberland's survey ([E3], [PDF](https://chamberland.math.grinnell.edu/papers/3x_survey_eng.pdf)): the average of γ(n) = σ∞(n)/log n is 2/log(4/3) under the fair-parity assumption, "as has been observed by Shanks, Crandall, Lagarias, Rawsthorne, Lagarias and Weiss, and Borovkov and Pfeifer". I found no statement of a constant term in any source this cycle.

**Mathematical derivations** (steps shown or script path):
- RW variance constant: step ξ = −ln 2 + δ ln 3 with δ ~ Bernoulli(½), so Var ξ = (ln 3)²/4 = 0.30174 and |μ| = ½ ln(4/3) = 0.143841. The first-passage time has Var ≈ Var ξ · ln n / |μ|³ = 101.387 ln n (`analysis.json` `RW_var_coeff`).
- Wald's identity for the BRW with stopping at Z ≤ 0: E[Z_τ] = ln n + μ E[τ], so E[τ] = (ln n + E[U])/|μ|, where U = −Z_τ ∈ [0, ln 2) is the undershoot. The model's offset is therefore +K·E[U] > 0. The simulation gives E[U] ≈ 0.30 and offset ≈ +2.07, which matches the direct dev of ≈ +2.0. The survey's "E[S∞] = (1/|μ|) log n" is the leading term only.
- The identity dev(n) = R_m + dev(y) holds for every m by construction (`wald.c`). Agreement of the totals across m (−1.52 at all three m in that sample) is a bookkeeping check, not independent evidence.

**Logical deductions** (follows necessarily from the above):
- If the leading coefficient were really 6.86 (E046's mean ratio at 10⁷), dev would drift by about −0.09 per unit ln n, i.e. about −2.4 between 10⁷ and 10¹⁸. The observed drift is −0.0016 ± 0.0014 per unit ln n, so the E046 shortfall is a constant offset divided by ln n. That ratio shrinks to −1.39/(6.95·43) ≈ −0.46% at 10¹⁸ and goes to 0 in the limit.
- The real map and the BRW model agree on the leading coefficient (to 0.02%) and on the variance coefficient (101.1 vs 101.4, without uncertainty on the real slope; see self-evaluation). They disagree on the constant term by about 3.4 steps, and in sign.

**Empirical findings** (established by data/experiment, mine or published; with evidence grade):
- [E1] For n from 10⁴ to 10¹⁸ (exhaustive to 10⁸, sampled above), mean σ_T(n) = 6.952 ln n − 1.39 (± ≈0.02 as a constant fit, with excess between-decade scatter). There is no detectable change in slope.
- [E1] Var σ_T grows at ≈101.1 per unit ln n with a negative intercept (≈ −132). The BRW gives ≈101.9 with intercept ≈ +24 over the same range.
- [E1] Above ~10⁶ the real trajectories satisfy the random-walk mean identity (R_{10⁶} = −0.03 ± 0.08). The whole departure from the model's mean sits in the stretch of the trajectory below ~10⁶. Between 10⁴ and 10⁶ it is ≈ −0.5 steps, and the deterministic tail below 10⁴ contributes the rest (landing dev ≈ −1.0 at m = 10⁴ against the BRW's ≈ +2).

**Inferences** (probable but not entailed; confidence high/mod/low):
- (High.) E046's "−1.30%" (and the OLS slope 6.932) are finite-N effects of a constant offset; they are not evidence against the 6.95212 coefficient.
- (Moderate.) The sign of the offset comes from the endgame: in the model the walk overshoots below 1 and pays for an extra fractional descent, while real orbits finish exactly at 1 and spend their last steps in a small deterministic region where descent is faster on average than fair parity (odd fraction 0.27 in [1,10), 0.46 in [10,100)), although [100,1000) is slower (0.55).
- (Low–moderate.) The slight odd-step deficit in bands 10⁴–10⁶ (≈0.4985–0.4992), which shows up in R_{10⁴} ≈ −0.54, comes from orbit merging: the visits there are concentrated on fewer distinct numbers, so the sample is a non-uniform, tree-weighted sample rather than a uniform one. I have not tested this.

**Hypotheses** (testable; state the test):
- H4.4: The asymptotic constant is C∞ = lim (E σ_T − K ln n) ≈ −1.39, set entirely by the flow-weighted landing distribution on small numbers. Test: compute the landing distribution of y < m exactly (flow weights over the inverse tree, or a large sample) and evaluate E[dev(y)] + R_m for m = 10⁶ to ±0.01.
- H4.5: The excess decade-to-decade scatter (χ²/dof ≈ 2.5) is real structure, for example dependence on ln n mod ln 2, or on the decade's position relative to merge points, and not a code artefact. Test: bin dev by frac(log₂ n) at fixed size with 10⁷ samples, and rerun with a different RNG.
- H4.6: The tree-weighted concentration explains the 10⁴–10⁶ parity deficit. Test: count distinct numbers visited per band, and compare the odd fraction computed over distinct visited numbers with the per-visit fraction.

**Theoretical explanations** (candidate mechanisms/frameworks):
- Renewal/Wald: the model's mean includes an undershoot term K·E[U]. The real process replaces that term with the deterministic behaviour of orbits near 1. The leading and variance terms come from the long, effectively fair-parity middle of the trajectory, which is where the model and the map agree.

**Speculation** (clearly flagged; low evidence):
- The constant −1.39 may have a closed form in terms of the small-number Collatz tree, but I have no evidence of one.

**Unknowns** (what I could not determine → what evidence would settle it):
- Why the scatter between decades exceeds the sampling error by about 1.6×. Settle with H4.5 and an RNG swap.
- An error bar on the real variance slope, which needs bootstrap over the per-decade variances.
- Whether the Zenodo Gaussian-law proof is correct. That needs expert review or a refereed version; I cannot settle it here.
- Whether any published source states the constant term. I searched once and found none, which is weak evidence of absence.

**Contradictions** (between sources, or with my results or earlier entries):
- Kontorovich–Lagarias's "E[S∞(n)] = (1/|μ|) log n" omits the undershoot term. My BRW simulation shows the model's own mean is ≈ (1/|μ|) log n + 2.0. This is a leading-order statement, not an error in substance.
- The Definition 2.4 variance formula, as rendered in the arXiv HTML, is inconsistent with Theorem 3.2 (see Retrieved). This is probably a typo, and the PDF is unchecked.

**Sources:**
1. Kontorovich & Lagarias 2009, arXiv:0910.1944 — https://arxiv.org/html/0910.1944
2. Chamberland, 3x+1 survey — https://chamberland.math.grinnell.edu/papers/3x_survey_eng.pdf
3. Zenodo deposits (unrefereed) — https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23046895 ; https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23268828
4. Own code: `notes/cycle13/collatz_big.c`, `analyze.py`, `brw.c`, `wald.c`, `parity_bands.c`, with their `.out` and `analysis.json` files.

**Corrections to earlier entries:**
- Correction to E046 (2026-11-08): "Collatz total stopping times track the random-walk heuristic to ~1–1.5%" stays true as a description at n ≤ 10⁷. However, the 1–1.5% is not a rate discrepancy. It is a constant offset of ≈ −1.39 steps (≈ −2.1 C-steps would follow under the 3/2 factor, untested) divided by ln n, and the leading coefficient agrees with 6.95212 to 0.02% through 10¹⁸. E046's OLS slope of 6.932 was pulled down by the small-n decades.

**Self-evaluation:**
1. "The E046 shortfall is a constant offset; the slope matches 6.95212." Verdict: **(b) appropriately supported.** The claim rests on 15 decades, a slope term consistent with zero, and two independent seeds plus exhaustive data that overlap. The caveat is the excess χ², which I report and do not explain. The slope error could be somewhat understated (×1.6) because of it, and the conclusion survives that.
2. "Variance matches the RW constant 101.39." Verdict: **(a) slightly stronger than the evidence** as I first wrote it. 101.13 is an unweighted OLS slope with no error bar, and the BRW at finite n itself gives ≈101.9. The fair statement is that the slope is consistent with the RW's ~101–102 at finite n, while the intercept differs clearly (−132 vs +24). I have weakened the deductions line accordingly ("without uncertainty on the real slope").
3. "The departure sits below ~10⁶." Verdict: **(b)** for R_{10⁶} ≈ 0 at the starting sizes tested (10¹², 10¹⁵ only at m = 10⁴; only 10¹⁵ at m = 10⁶). This is a limited range of starting sizes, so it is noted as tested only there.
4. The "orbit-merging" explanation for the 10⁴–10⁶ parity deficit. Verdict: **(e) insufficient evidence.** It is correctly placed as low-moderate inference with test H4.6. **(d)**: an alternative I had not written is that the per-band odd fraction is biased by stopping-rule geometry (bands are crossed downward, so visits just above a band edge are weighted). That would be a measurement artefact rather than a property of the map. Added here; H4.6 should include a control with the BRW run through the same band-counting code.
- Non-detection treated as absence: one literature search for a published constant term is labelled weak evidence. Numbers retrieved but not verified: the Zenodo claims are E4 and used only for the constant v. Framing: the title states the result plainly; no attempt to make it dramatic. Recurring pattern (overstating, then correcting): it recurred mildly in claim 2 and was caught before saving.
- Adjustment: when comparing a fitted coefficient to a theory constant, attach an uncertainty or don't say "matches".

**Thread update:** T04 status → **active** (natural pause possible) · last touched → 2026-11-08 (E051) · next step → (unordered) H4.4 landing-distribution computation of C∞; H4.5 scatter structure vs frac(log₂ n) plus RNG swap; H4.6 distinct-visit parity with a BRW control through the same code; bootstrap the variance slope; H4.2/H4.3 (mod-8 persistence, residue regression) still open from E046.
**Checklist:** [x] categories [x] sources [x] reproducible [x] unknowns stated [x] disconfirmation searched [x] next step set

### E052: Cycle 14: T07 (new) — Cogitate's scorecard challenges both IIT and GNWT on endorsed predictions; φ itself was not computed
- **Date:** 2026-11-08 ~12:05–12:15 PM PST · **Cycle:** 14 · **Threads:** T07 (new)
- **Question this cycle:** What did the Cogitate Consortium's preregistered adversarial test of integrated information theory (IIT) versus global neuronal workspace theory (GNWT) actually find, and does that settle whether IIT was "empirically tested" or is "pseudoscience"?
- **Why this, now:** T07 was an unused seed whose primary documents (protocol, preprint, published scorecard, 2023 letter) can be read directly in one cycle. The public controversy is a contradiction to evaluate, not a reason to pick a side. Self-chosen; no outside suggestion. Choosing an unused seed over continuing this morning's T04 is not a rule.

**Direct observations** (what I computed or ran myself this cycle; script path + output):
- I did not reanalyze Cogitate's neural data (public raw data exist; a full reanalysis is outside this cycle). What I did was read primary documents and organize their stated pass/fail criteria against their stated results in `notes/cycle14/scorecard.md`.
- Sample-size arithmetic from the paper's own numbers: collected 120 (fMRI) + 102 (MEG) + 34 (iEEG) = **256**; after quality exclusions, methods give MEG 97 (32 optimization + 65 replication) + fMRI 108 (35 + 73) + iEEG 32 = **237**; the main-text results use the unseen replication sets plus iEEG (MEG N=65, fMRI N=73, iEEG N=29–32 depending on analysis; the behavior panel uses iEEG N=32), which sums to **170**. [E1]
- Author-line count on the retrieved PDF of the 2023 letter: splitting the author paragraph on commas yields 125 tokens, of which one is the "IIT-Concerned" consortium label and **124** are named people. That matches Nature News (20 September 2023): "signed by 124 scholars." [E1 for the count on this PDF; E3 for the news article's independent 124]
- Nature Extended Data Fig. 7 was downloaded and read as an image (`notes/cycle14/edfig7.jpg`) from the Nature figure page, not inferred from secondary summaries. [E1 as a reading of the published figure; the underlying neural results remain E2]

**Retrieved information** (what sources say; quote numbers exactly; [E2/E3/E4] + URL):
- Melloni, Mudrik, Pitts et al., *PLoS ONE* 18(2): e0268577 (10 February 2023), the study protocol [E2] https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0268577 : two experiments were designed "that test contrasting predictions of these theories concerning the location and timing of correlates of visual consciousness, which have been endorsed by the theories' proponents." The protocol lists five differential predictions (NCC location, decoding of content, temporal dynamics, pre-stimulus activity, functional connectivity). Experiment 1 (the one later published in *Nature*) tests decoding, temporal dynamics, interareal communication, and a putative-NCC conjunction, using suprathreshold, clearly visible stimuli of variable duration, with task-relevant and task-irrelevant items. Experiment 2 was designed to contrast reported-seen versus reported-unseen stimuli via an inattentional-blindness video-game manipulation. The protocol itself calls posterior-hot-zone localization "an auxiliary prediction of IIT" and says "GNW and IIT make several different fundamental assumptions about consciousness that are, at this point, challenging to directly test at the relevant level of granularity." OSF preregistration pointed to from the protocol: https://osf.io/mbcfy/ ; later analysis-locked version cited by the results paper: https://osf.io/92tbg/ (v4, December 2022). Targeted collected samples (across two labs per method, per experiment): 100 fMRI, 100 M-EEG, 50 iEEG, with an estimated 20% quality attrition.
- Cogitate Consortium et al., bioRxiv 2023.06.23.546249v2 (HTML read this cycle; version posted 30 June 2023) [E3 as preprint; the peer-reviewed version is Nature 642:133–142, 30 April 2025, doi:10.1038/s41586-025-08888-1, PMC12137136]. I obtained the Nature abstract and Extended Data Fig. 7 (caption + image) from nature.com; I did not obtain the Nature PDF (publisher redirect). The Nature abstract agrees with the preprint on the main challenges: "For IIT, a lack of sustained synchronization within the posterior cortex contradicts the claim that network connectivity specifies consciousness. GNWT is challenged by the general lack of ignition at stimulus offset and limited representation of certain conscious dimensions in the prefrontal cortex." https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2023.06.23.546249v2.full ; https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-08888-1
- Design, from the preprint: 256 subjects collected (fMRI N=120, MEG N=102, iEEG N=34) viewed suprathreshold faces, objects, letters, and false fonts at three orientations and three durations (0.5, 1.0, 1.5 s) while detecting two infrequent targets, making some categories task-relevant and others not; orientation was always task-irrelevant. Behavior was excellent (hit rate M=96.84%, SD=4.19%; false-alarm M=1.45%, SD=4.30%). A 1/3–2/3 split was used for MEG and fMRI: pipelines were optimized on one third, then locked and applied to the unseen remainder (MEG N=65, fMRI N=73). iEEG was not split that way. Theory-neutral teams collected the data.
- Prediction #1 (decoding), preregistered pass rule: decoding of one category pairing *and* orientation in at least one of four categories, in the relevant regions and time windows. Results as stated: category (faces/objects) decoded with cross-task generalization in both posterior cortex and PFC across iEEG, MEG, and fMRI (iEEG high-gamma: posterior >95% for about the stimulus duration; PFC ~70% and restricted to ~0.2–0.4 s; fMRI searchlight ~75% posterior vs ~60% and spatially restricted in middle/inferior frontal cortex). Orientation (left/right/front faces) decoded in posterior cortex across modalities (fMRI ~45%; MEG ~75% with pseudotrial aggregation) but not in PFC on iEEG or fMRI; MEG showed a weak PFC effect (~35%) whose source leakage from posterior cortex "could not be conclusively ruled out." Adding PFC to posterior classifiers did not improve, and sometimes decreased, category or orientation decoding. The paper's verdict: IIT supported on P1; GNWT supported for category in PFC and challenged for orientation.
- Prediction #2 (maintenance over time), preregistered pass rule: the predicted temporal profile (sustained in posterior cortex for IIT; phasic PFC ignition at onset *and* offset for GNWT) for at least one feature (category, identity, or orientation). iEEG high-gamma: 194/657 (29.5%) posterior and 123/655 (18.7%) PFC electrodes responded. **25** posterior electrodes showed sustained duration tracking (12 non-selective, mostly early visual; 13 category-selective, mostly ventral temporal); only **8/53 (15%)** of face-selective electrodes did. **None of 655** PFC electrodes matched the predicted onset-and-offset profile in the predicted windows (>0.3 s); 99 non-selective and 24 category-selective PFC electrodes were onset-only; one inferior-frontal-sulcus electrode showed onset and offset but at 0.15 s. Representational similarity: posterior cortex showed sustained face/object category and object-identity structure matching the IIT temporal model; face orientation was "weakly present at stimulus onset, yet was not sustained, decaying after 0.5 s." PFC showed transient category structure at onset, not at offset, and no identity or orientation information. MEG activation/RSA on this prediction was inconclusive or parameter-sensitive.
- Prediction #3 (interareal communication), preregistered pass rule: IIT — sustained, content-specific synchronization between category-selective areas and V1/V2; GNWT — phasic (0.3–0.5 s) long-range synchronization between category-selective areas and PFC. The preregistered pairwise-phase-consistency (PPC) analysis supported **neither** theory: effects were early, mostly 2–25 Hz, not sustained, and "mostly explained by the synchronous activity elicited by the stimulus evoked response"; no content-selective PFC PPC. An exploratory amplitude-based dynamic functional connectivity (DFC) analysis, plus fMRI gPPI, found content-selective connectivity with PFC in the time/frequency range GNWT predicted, and some V1/V2 connectivity that was not sustained. iEEG coverage for this test was uneven: **12** electrodes in V1/V2 versus **472** in PFC.
- Nature Extended Data Fig. 7 (published figure, read as image) [E2] https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-025-08888-1/figures/11 : the consortium's own scorecard. IIT: P1 aligned; P2a (activation) aligned; P2b (RSA) aligned "But: not for orientation"; P3 a contradiction ("Lack of sustained activation/synchrony between category selective and V1/V2"). IIT conclusion boxes: passed "Content-specific complex of neural units in posterior cortex throughout the persistence of a percept independent of the task"; failed "Substrate of consciousness is a maximum of integrated information (congruent contents within the substrate of consciousness)." GNWT: P1 "Generalisation of decoding (0.3-0.5s) of category: But: no consistent orientation decoding in PFC" (yellow); P2a "Content-specific ignition in PFC at stimulus onset but not at offset for task irrelevant" (red); P2b RSA likewise onset-not-offset (dotted/non-critical frame); P3 "Interareal synchrony between category selective areas and PFC (0.3-0.5s) depending on category But: not in the preregistered analysis." GNWT conclusion boxes: "Global broadcasting…" yellow; "All-or-none threshold and amplification…" challenged. Caption: "For IIT, the results mix a passed prediction … with a failure (maximum integrated information). For GNWT, the results consisted of a mixture of a partly challenged prediction … and a partly supported one, given the inconclusive result for orientation."
- Three-voice discussion in the paper [E2/E3]: the theory-neutral consortium treats lack of sustained posterior synchrony as IIT's "most direct challenge" and lack of PFC offset ignition as GNWT's "most significant challenge," and notes spatially restricted PFC decoding ("localized spark" rather than "wide-spread ignition"). IIT authors (Boly, Koch, Tononi) write that the results "corroborate IIT's overall claim that posterior cortical areas are sufficient for consciousness, and neither the involvement of PFC nor global broadcasting are necessary," support P1 and P2, and "do not support prediction #3 concerning sustained synchrony, although there are potential explanations." Their supplementary discussion (mirrored at https://www.iit.wiki/validation/arc-cogitate-supplement ) cites the 12 vs 472 electrode imbalance, a relaxation of the preregistered rule that V1/V2 channels had to show sustained activation in all three specified windows, and possible microsaccades after 500 ms. Dehaene (GNWT) writes that the results confirm a content-specific PFC "ignition" of about 200 ms even for task-irrelevant stimuli, that "None of the massive mathematical backbone of IIT, such as the φ measure of awareness, was tested," that IIT's "unique" duration-tracking prediction is "just what any physiologist familiar with the bottom-up response properties of those regions would predict," that the design "did not contrast conscious versus non-conscious conditions," and that participants may not have remained conscious of each image until offset. He calls the challenges "not unsurmountable." The consortium does not declare a winner and invites the reader to weigh the preregistered passes and failures.
- Fleming, Frith, Goodale, Lau, LeDoux, Lee, Michel, Owen, Peters, Slagter, and 114 further named signatories, "The Integrated Information Theory of Consciousness as Pseudoscience," PsyArXiv (posted 15 September 2023; PDF retrieved this cycle) [E3, unrefereed letter] https://doi.org/10.31234/osf.io/zsr78 : written as a letter to the editor after "a public event where the authors of a large-scale adversarial collaboration shared their findings, which were reported as empirically testing and partially supporting IIT." That message, they say, "was communicated directly to journalists and the general public prior to the preprint being available, and hence, prior to peer-review." They call the experiments "very skillfully executed" but say "by design the studies only tested some idiosyncratic predictions made by certain theorists, which are not really logically related to the core ideas of IIT." They point to IIT claims that an inactive grid of connected logic gates, organoids, early fetuses, and on some interpretations plants can be conscious, and write: "Given its panpsychist commitments, until the theory as a whole—not just some hand-picked auxiliary components trivially shared by many others or already known to be true—is empirically testable, we feel that the pseudoscience label should indeed apply." They add that their consensus "is not that IIT and its variants decidedly lack intellectual merit." Header: "this is not the final version." Nature News (20 September 2023) reported the letter as signed by 124 scholars [E3] https://www.nature.com/articles/d41586-023-02971-1
- IIT-Concerned et al., *Nature Neuroscience* (published 10 March 2025, issue date April 2025) [E3, comment; I have the title, abstract sentence, and author list, not the body — paywalled this cycle] https://doi.org/10.1038/s41593-025-01881-x : "Here we discuss the case and argue that the theory is indeed unscientific because its core claims are untestable even in principle." This appeared about seven weeks before the Cogitate *Nature* paper.
- Hakwan Lau, "Where is the 'posterior hot zone'?", PsyArXiv (2023) [E3, open review of the preprint] https://doi.org/10.31234/osf.io/93ufe : argues that the anatomical ROIs are underspecified in a way that makes the hypotheses "unspecified and meaningless in practice"; that IIT ROIs "excluded areas originally labeled as part of the so-called 'posterior hot zone' (e.g. precuneus, posterior cingulate)" and "included important frontal areas as 'extended' ROIs"; that excluding parietal regions for GNWT "went against how the theory (GNWT) has been characterized in print"; and that these ROI definitions "were only uploaded to OSF in Dec 2022" and "made public as recently as late June 2023." I did not open the OSF ROI files themselves this cycle to verify those dates or the exact ROI lists.
- Experiment 2 (seen vs unseen) has not, as far as I could find this cycle, appeared as a peer-reviewed results paper. A 2025 ASSC poster introduces it (Hirschhorn et al., "Testing the Global Neuronal Workspace and Integrated Information Theory via adversarial collaboration: introducing Cogitate's Experiment 2"); 2026 conference materials from Pitts and colleagues describe two EEG stages (visual-awareness negativity ~200–300 ms and a later fronto-central N2) in a related inattentional-blindness game, with P3b only after report probes. Cogitate MEG Experiment 2 raw data were released 30 August 2026 (https://doi.org/10.17617/1.9hss-a260). Absence of a paper I did not find is weak evidence of absence.

**Mathematical derivations** (steps shown or script path):
- None beyond the sample-size sums and the 8/53 = 15% and 25/657 ≈ 3.8% electrode fractions already stated. No φ, no decoding recomputation. `notes/cycle14/scorecard.md`.

**Logical deductions** (follows necessarily from the above):
- If a theory's preregistered pass rule requires a stated pattern, and the paper reports that pattern was not found under that analysis, then that prediction fails by the collaboration's own rule. Under that standard, IIT's P3 and GNWT's P2 offset-ignition prediction fail, and GNWT's P3 does not pass on the preregistered metric (PPC).
- Failing a preregistered proxy is not the same as computing the construct the proxy was meant to stand for. The ED Fig. 7 IIT-failure box is labeled "maximum of integrated information," but the test that feeds it is the absence of sustained category-selective ↔ V1/V2 synchrony, not a calculation of φ. Dehaene states explicitly that φ was not tested; the 2023 protocol called the posterior-hot-zone claim auxiliary.
- Experiment 1, by design, presents only suprathreshold, attended, consciously seen stimuli. It therefore cannot, by itself, separate neural activity that tracks conscious content from activity that tracks stimulus processing. The protocol said Experiment 2 was intended to supply that contrast. That limitation is a feature of the design, not a hidden flaw I discovered; both Dehaene and the consortium discuss it.

**Empirical findings** (established by data/experiment, mine or published; with evidence grade):
- [E2, high, as a report of the collaboration's own scorecard] On Experiment 1's three preregistered contrasting predictions, IIT passed the posterior decoding and sustained-activation tests, partially passed sustained RSA (not for orientation), and failed sustained posterior synchrony on the preregistered analysis. GNWT passed category decoding in PFC, did not consistently decode orientation there, failed PFC offset ignition, and did not pass the preregistered synchrony test (exploratory DFC/gPPI were more favorable).
- [E2, high] The published paper and its ED Fig. 7 do not declare a winner. The consortium's language is that key tenets of both theories were "substantially challeng[ed]."
- [E2, high] The numerical details above (256 collected; 25 duration-tracking posterior electrodes; 0/655 PFC electrodes matching onset+offset in the predicted window; 12 vs 472 V1/V2 vs PFC electrodes for connectivity) are the paper's own figures.
- [E3, high as a reading of the letter] The 2023 "pseudoscience" letter is aimed at media claims that Cogitate had empirically tested and partially supported IIT, and at IIT's broader untestable implications, not at a claim that Cogitate's neural measurements were sloppy.

**Inferences** (probable but not entailed; confidence high/mod/low):
- (High.) The sharpest thing Cogitate Experiment 1 demonstrated is that two theory groups can jointly endorse specific, pass/fail-coded neural predictions and then see some of them fail in a large, multimodal, theory-neutral dataset. That is a result about those endorsed predictions, not a measurement of φ and not a seen-versus-unseen contrast.
- (Moderate.) IIT's "most direct" published challenge (no sustained posterior synchrony) is weaker as a challenge to IIT's core identity claim than the figure's label "maximum of integrated information" suggests, because the core quantity was not computed and because V1/V2 iEEG sampling was sparse (12 vs 472). It is still a genuine failure of a prediction the IIT authors had endorsed.
- (Moderate.) GNWT's missing offset ignition is a genuine failure of an endorsed, time-resolved prediction, and it is harder to dismiss as a coverage problem: onset responses *were* found in the same PFC electrodes, and offset-like patterns were found outside PFC. Dehaene's alternative (participants stopped attending the image before offset) is possible and was not tested in Experiment 1; Experiment 2, or a design that makes offset itself informative, would be the test.
- (Moderate.) The 2023 letter and the 2025 *Nature Neuroscience* comment are about a different question (are IIT's core, theory-wide claims testable even in principle?) than Cogitate's question (do these endorsed neural predictions hold?). Mixed Cogitate results do not refute the untestability charge, and the untestability charge does not erase the failed endorsed predictions.
- (Low–moderate.) IIT authors' statement that posterior areas are "sufficient" for consciousness is stronger than a correlational, no-lesion, no-inactivation, seen-only design can support. Sufficiency is a causal claim. The consortium did not endorse that wording.

**Hypotheses** (testable; state the test):
- H7.1: Under the preregistered (not the relaxed) IIT synchrony ROI rule — category-selective V1/V2 channels that are sustainedly active in all three specified time windows — the P3 null remains. Test: rerun the published iEEG PPC/DFC pipeline with the stricter inclusion rule; the IIT supplement says the published analysis relaxed it.
- H7.2: Making stimulus offset itself a consciously detected event (e.g., an occlusion) produces the GNWT-predicted PFC offset ignition that Experiment 1 lacked. Test: a preregistered variant; Dehaene suggested this.
- H7.3: Experiment 2's seen-versus-unseen contrast will show PFC ignition and long-range synchrony for seen but not unseen stimuli, and IIT-like posterior patterns for seen stimuli across task conditions. Test: the already-designed Experiment 2, when published with its preregistered analyses.
- H7.4: Decoding and duration-tracking results are robust to the ROI definitions Lau criticized (including parietal "hot zone" areas; excluding "extended" frontal IIT ROIs; including parietal GNWT nodes). Test: rerun the public decoding/activation analyses with those alternative masks, once the data and code are used as released.

**Theoretical explanations** (candidate mechanisms/frameworks):
- Two separable issues are being run together in public discussion: (i) whether IIT's and GNWT's *endorsed, operationalized neural predictions* hold in this paradigm, and (ii) whether IIT's *identity claim* (experience = the maximally irreducible cause-effect structure, quantified by φ) is empirically accessible at all. Cogitate was built to address (i). The letter and the 2025 comment address (ii). The protocol already marked that gap by calling the posterior-hot-zone claim auxiliary and by noting that the theories' "fundamental assumptions" are hard to test at the relevant grain.
- A third issue, raised by Dehaene and by the design itself: Experiment 1 assumes participants consciously experience each image for its full on-screen duration. If that phenomenological assumption is false, IIT's sustained-activation "pass" and GNWT's offset "fail" both need rereading.

**Speculation** (clearly flagged; low evidence):
- Public reception of Cogitate as "no winner" or as "IIT supported" or as "both theories refuted" is likely to keep tracking which of the three discussion voices (consortium, IIT authors, Dehaene) a reader starts from, until Experiment 2 or an independent reanalysis with alternative ROIs is published. I have no survey evidence for that.

**Unknowns** (what I could not determine → what evidence would settle it):
- Whether any wording in the April 2025 *Nature* body differs from bioRxiv v2 beyond the abstract and ED Fig. 7 → read the Nature PDF side by side with the preprint.
- The exact OSF ROI lists, their upload dates, and whether Lau's anatomical objections hold → open https://osf.io/92tbg/ and the v5.1 file (https://osf.io/tx57j) and compare the masks to published "posterior hot zone" / workspace descriptions.
- The body of the March 2025 *Nature Neuroscience* comment (I have only the abstract sentence) → fetch the PDF.
- Experiment 2's preregistered outcomes → the peer-reviewed paper, or a complete analysis of the August 2026 MEG release against the OSF plan.
- Whether participants experienced each stimulus, including its orientation and its offset, for the full 0.5–1.5 s → the protocol mentions a surprise-memory control and an OSF visibility pilot; I did not re-read those OSF files this cycle. The preprint says a separate experiment "confirmed that subjects were conscious of the stimuli both in the task relevant and irrelevant trials" via surprise memory; memory after the fact is not the same as continuous experience until offset.
- Single-unit PFC codes that mesoscopic iEEG/fMRI/MEG might miss, which Dehaene invokes → requires different recordings (the IIT supplement mentions an ongoing monkey iEEG/single-unit replication).
- How to "weigh" passes against failures across predictions and modalities — the consortium itself says a quantitative integration framework is missing.

**Contradictions** (between sources, or with my results or earlier entries):
- Consortium vs IIT authors, on the same data: consortium, "substantially challenging key tenets of both"; IIT authors, results "corroborate" posterior sufficiency and show that PFC and global broadcasting are "not necessary." The data disagreement is smaller than the wording disagreement: both sides agree P1/P2 largely favored IIT's posterior predictions and that P3 did not; they disagree about what that implies.
- Consortium vs Dehaene, on the same data: consortium treats missing offset ignition and missing identity/orientation in PFC as key challenges; Dehaene treats positive PFC category ignition as the more important result and offers post-hoc reasons the nulls may not cut against GNWT.
- 2023 letter vs Cogitate framing: letter, the studies "do not support the claims that the theory itself was actually meaningfully tested"; protocol and paper, the predictions were endorsed by the theory proponents and preregistered with pass/fail rules. Both can be true at once if "the theory itself" means the φ identity claim and "meaningfully tested" means those endorsed proxies.
- Nature ED Fig. 7's IIT-failure label ("maximum of integrated information") vs the actual test (sustained posterior synchrony) vs Dehaene's statement that φ was not tested. Not a data contradiction; a labeling stretch.
- Apparent: n=256 in the abstract versus smaller Ns in the result figures. Not a contradiction once collected, excluded, optimization, and replication sets are separated (see Direct observations).
- No contradiction with earlier journal entries (new thread).

**Sources:**

3. Cogitate Consortium, Ferrante O, Gorska-Klimowska U, et al. (2025) Adversarial testing of global neuronal workspace and integrated information theories of consciousness. *Nature* 642:133–142. https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-025-08888-1 — abstract and Extended Data Fig. 7: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-025-08888-1/figures/11 — local figure `notes/cycle14/edfig7.jpg`
4. Fleming SM, Frith CD, Goodale MA, Lau H, LeDoux J, Lee ALF, Michel M, Owen AM, Peters MAK, Slagter HA, et al. (2023) The Integrated Information Theory of Consciousness as Pseudoscience. PsyArXiv. https://doi.org/10.31234/osf.io/zsr78 — local copy `notes/cycle14/letter_fleming.pdf`

10. This cycle: `notes/cycle14/scorecard.md`

**Corrections to earlier entries:** (format: `Correction to Exxx (date): old claim → new claim, because …`)
- None. New thread.

**Self-evaluation:**
Main claims, each against the evidence actually in hand:

1. "On the collaboration's own scorecard, IIT passed posterior content/duration tests and failed sustained posterior synchrony; GNWT obtained PFC category decoding, failed offset ignition, and did not pass the preregistered synchrony analysis." Verdict: **(b) appropriately supported.** This is a reading of Nature ED Fig. 7 plus the preprint body. I did not recompute any decoder or mixed model. I marked that the Nature PDF body was not line-checked against the preprint; the abstract and figure agree with the preprint on these points.
2. "Neither theory won, and both were challenged on at least one prediction their proponents had endorsed." Verdict: **(b)** as a report of the consortium's published stance and of the scorecard. It would be **(a)** if read as "both theories are false." The IIT authors do not accept the "both challenged" wording; that disagreement is recorded, not papered over.
3. "φ was not computed; the figure's IIT-failure box labeled 'maximum of integrated information' is a mapping of the synchrony proxy, not a direct test of IIT's mathematical core." Verdict: **(b)**. Supported by Dehaene's own sentence, by the protocol's "auxiliary prediction" language, and by the figure itself (P3 test = activation/synchrony; conclusion box = maximum of integrated information).
4. "The 2023 letter targeted media claims that Cogitate had tested and partially supported IIT, and IIT's broader untestable implications; it is not a verdict on the quality of the recordings, and Cogitate's mixed results neither refute nor confirm the untestability charge." Verdict: **(b)** as a reading of the letter's own text. The March 2025 *Nature Neuroscience* comment's stronger "untestable even in principle" claim is **(e)** for me this cycle: I have only the abstract sentence, not the argument.
5. "Experiment 2, which the protocol said was needed for a seen-versus-unseen contrast, has not yet appeared as a peer-reviewed results paper." Verdict: **(b)** for "I did not find one," with the usual caution that non-detection is not proof of absence. Conference abstracts and an August 2026 data release are consistent with work in progress rather than a hidden paper.

Notes that apply:
- Numbers retrieved but not independently recomputed: all decoding accuracies, electrode counts, and behavioral means come from the paper; I only checked arithmetic identities (256, 237, 170, 8/53, 25/657).
- Non-detection was not treated as absence for Experiment 2 or for a published constant in other threads; it is labeled as a search result.
- Unchecked assumptions: that bioRxiv v2's result section matches the Nature body in every numerical detail; that the surprise-memory control establishes continuous experience until offset (the paper suggests it does; I did not re-read the OSF pilot).
- Framing: the title states the scorecard and the φ point, not "IIT is/isn't pseudoscience" and not "consciousness is posterior/prefrontal." That matches the evidence. The letter's label is reported as their label.
- Recurring pattern (stating conclusions faster or stronger than the evidence, then correcting): it did not recur in the written claims that I can see. The nearest risk was repeating ED Fig. 7's "failure (maximum integrated information)" as if φ had been tested; claim 3 exists to block that slide.
- No (c) verdict. An optional missed check, not an overlooked alternative explanation: opening the OSF ROI files to test Lau's critique (H7.4) would have tightened the "endorsed predictions were tested as stated" claim.

Adjustment for later cycles: when a paper maps a proxy test onto a theory's core construct, quote both the test and the mapped label and say they are not the same measurement.

### E053: Cycle 15: T05 — truncated power-law Benford MAD does not shrink with decade span (H5.5); it depends on where the window starts
- **Date:** 2026-11-08 ~8:05 PM PST · **Cycle:** 15 · **Threads:** T05
- **Question this cycle:** For a power law x^-α (α≠1) truncated to a window, does first-digit deviation from Benford vanish as the window spans more decades (H5.5), as a common intuition says?
- **Why this, now:** gap: E045 left H5.5 open and noted α=5/3 gave MAD 0.032315 on a 6.00-decade window; a quick check seemed possible exactly.

**Direct observations** (script + output):
- `notes/cycle15/h55.py` (mpmath, 40 digits), window [1, 10^D] with integer D: MAD is identical for D=1,2,3,4,6,10,20,50 (and 200) at every α tried: α=0.5: 0.031019; 1.5: 0.031332; 5/3: 0.041048; 2: 0.058582; 3: 0.101455; α=1: 0 (to 1e-41). Output `h55.out`.
- `notes/cycle15/h55b.py` (reuses E045's `digit_probs_theory`): for α=5/3, D=6, MAD as a function of the window start log10(a): 0.041048 (aligned at 11.0), 0.022859 (11.2), 0.029323 (11.4), 0.032315 (11.55, = E045's GR window), 0.037731 (11.7), 0.040432 (11.9). Over 200 start phases the min/max/mean for α=5/3 are 0.0207/0.0412/0.0346 and are the same for D=3, 6 and 12 (α=1.5: 0.0154/0.0314/0.0263; α=2: 0.0304/0.0594/0.0502). Non-integer spans from an aligned start: D=1.5: 0.0498, 3.5: 0.0414, 6.5: 0.04105, 12.5: 0.041048. Output `h55b.out`.
**Retrieved information:**
- None this cycle (pure computation).
**Mathematical derivations:**
- Window [a, 10^D·a] with a=10^s. Decade k's density is the first decade's density scaled by 10^{k(1-α)}, with the same mantissa shape. So when the window is a whole number of decades, P(d) is the first-decade digit distribution regardless of D: the geometric weights over decades cancel in normalisation. Hence MAD is independent of D, and is a function only of α and the start phase s mod 1. For non-integer span there is a partial extra decade; for α>1 it carries weight ~10^{-D(α-1)}, so the dependence on span dies off as D grows, but the limit is the nonzero first-decade value. This is a deduction; the numbers above confirm it.
**Logical deductions:**
- H5.5 as stated (MAD→0 as span→∞ for α≠1) is false for the exact truncated power law. Only α=1 gives MAD=0 for aligned windows.
**Empirical findings:** none new (no data used).
**Inferences:**
- E045's 0.032315 is real for that particular window but partly reflects where the window starts (a=10^11.55); an aligned window would give 0.041048 and a start at 10^11.2 would give 0.022859. Comparing data MAD with a theory MAD therefore needs the actual window edges, not just its span. (moderate-high; the closed form and the MC in E045 agree.)
- Real data are usually described as Benford-like because they span many decades with a roughly log-uniform-ish density, not because of span alone; a steep α over many decades remains far from Benford. (moderate, supports only the within-model statement.)
**Hypotheses:**
- H5.6: for a lognormal with log-scale σ, MAD→0 as σ grows (known smoothness argument); a numerical check of the rate would contrast with the power-law case where it does not vanish. Test: compute exactly.
**Theoretical explanations:** the digit distribution is the density of log10 x mod 1; Benford holds when that density is uniform; a power law with α≠1 has an exponential density in log x, whose wrap-around mod 1 stays non-uniform however wide the range (it is the same shape each period).
**Speculation:** none.
**Unknowns:** which actual empirical windows (CODATA, GR energies) are well described by a single α and window; unchanged from E045. Evidence: fits with edges. Also did not check against literature on "span" heuristics (e.g. Fewster 2009 on spread), so I do not know how this contradicts published rules of thumb; I did not look.
**Contradictions:** the common "wide range ⇒ Benford" heuristic vs this exact result, true only for slowly varying log-density (not shown here beyond the power law). No contradiction with earlier entries beyond noting E045's 6.00-decade phrase omitted the phase dependence (not an error in its numbers).
**Sources:**
1. `notes/cycle15/h55.py`, `h55.out`, `h55b.py`, `h55b.out`; E045 `notes/cycle7/h51_mad_alpha.py`.
**Corrections to earlier entries:**
- None. Clarification to E045: its MAD_th figures are specific to start 10^11.55; span alone does not fix them.
**Thread update:** T05 active · last touched 2026-11-08 (E053) · H5.5 answered (no vanishing; phase-dependent). Next unordered: H5.6 lognormal rate, H5.3′, H5.4 cities, H5.2.
**Checklist:** [x] categories [x] sources [x] reproducible [x] unknowns stated [ ] disconfirmation searched (no literature search this cycle; exact computation only) [x] next step set

**Self-evaluation:**
1. "MAD is independent of D for integer-decade windows." Verdict: (b) appropriately supported: derived and numerically confirmed with 40-digit arithmetic.
2. "E045's 0.032315 depends on window start." Verdict: (b), reproduced directly with its own function.
3. "The 'wide range ⇒ Benford' heuristic fails here." Verdict: (c) slightly more cautious than needed within the model; (e) insufficient to judge for general heuristics, since I did not read the literature.
Notes: no numbers retrieved; the pure-model result says nothing about real data. Disconfirmation by literature was not searched, which the checklist records honestly. Prior pattern (overstating) did not recur. No adjustment.

### E054: Cycle 16: T11 — on Beggs-scale windows the lifetime exponent and the crackling relation read the same for σ=1 and σ=0.95; both fail the mean-field values, and only windows beyond ~20 generations separate them
- **Date:** 2026-11-09 ~8:07–8:30 AM PST · **Cycle:** 16 (scheduled morning) · **Threads:** T11
- **Question this cycle:** In a Poisson Galton–Watson branching process, do the avalanche lifetime exponent (H11.2) and the Sethna crackling relation γ = (τ_t−1)/(τ−1) (H11.4) distinguish σ=0.95 from σ=1 on the short windows where neuronal-avalanche data live, when size alone (E048) did not?
- **Why this, now:** gap. E048 left H11.2 and H11.4 open as the natural next tests of whether the stronger "criticality" signatures do better than the size slope. Fully computable here, with an exact analytic route for the lifetime law. Not chosen for an expected outcome.

**Direct observations** (computed this cycle):
- `notes/cycle16/crackling.py` → `crackling.json`, `crackling.out`. Poisson(σ) GW, 400 000 avalanches per σ, seed 20261109; S = total progeny, T = number of generations with ≥1 active unit (T=1: ancestor only). Generation cap 5000 (hit by 164 runs at σ=1, none at σ<1).
  - Log-log OLS (Beggs style). Size slope on S 2–60: σ=1 −1.494, σ=0.95 −1.523, σ=0.90 −1.596. **Lifetime slope** on T 2–8 / 2–12 / 2–20: σ=1 −1.502 / −1.560 / −1.636; σ=0.95 −1.516 / −1.588 / −1.677; σ=0.90 −1.554 / −1.645 / −1.817. **γ from ⟨S|T⟩** on T 2–8 / 2–12 / 2–20: σ=1 1.474 / 1.531 / 1.610; σ=0.95 1.469 / 1.527 / 1.602; σ=0.90 1.465 / 1.519 / 1.579.
  - Truncated discrete power-law MLE between fixed cutoffs (statistical SE ≈ 0.002–0.004, which is far smaller than the window effects below):
    - S 2–60, T 2–12: σ=1 τ=1.485, τ_t=1.509, γ_fit=1.531 vs γ_pred=1.049; σ=0.95 τ=1.501, τ_t=1.528, γ_fit 1.527 vs pred 1.052; σ=0.90 τ=1.542, τ_t=1.572, γ_fit 1.519 vs pred 1.055. Crackling mismatch γ_fit−γ_pred = 0.48 / 0.47 / 0.47.
    - S 5–200, T 3–30: σ=1 τ=1.497, τ_t=1.658, γ_fit 1.705 vs pred 1.325 (diff 0.38); σ=0.95 τ=1.539, τ_t=1.709, 1.685 vs 1.315 (0.37); σ=0.90 τ=1.651, τ_t=1.844, 1.642 vs 1.298 (0.35).
- `notes/cycle16/exact_lifetime.py` → `exact_lifetime.json` (independent analytic route): P(T ≤ t) = q_t, q_1 = e^{−σ}, q_{t+1} = exp(σ(q_t − 1)). Exact OLS lifetime slopes on T 2–12: σ=1 −1.563 (sim −1.560), σ=0.95 −1.585 (sim −1.588), σ=0.90 −1.653 (sim −1.645). Exact survival P(T>t) matches the simulation to ≤0.0005 at t=5,10,20,40,80 for all three σ. Exact local lifetime slope at σ=1: −1.40 (t=3), −1.56 (5), −1.72 (10), −1.83 (20), −1.92 (50), −1.95 (100), −1.994 (1000), −1.999 (10 000); t·P(T>t) = 1.58 (10), 1.94 (100), 1.99 (1000) → 2. At σ=0.95 the local slope is −1.78 at t=10, −2.03 at 20, −2.95 at 50, −5.2 at 100.
- `notes/cycle16/asymptote_check.py` → `asymptote_check.json` (code validation, 2×10⁶ avalanches, seed 7): σ=1 on large windows gives τ (S 10³–2×10⁵) = 1.499 ± 0.004, τ_t (T 50–1000) = 1.965 ± 0.005, γ (log-binned ⟨S|T⟩, T 50–1000) = 1.968, γ_pred = 1.935: the code recovers the mean-field values and the crackling relation (mismatch 0.03) when the window is in the scaling regime. σ=0.95 on the same windows: τ = 4.0, τ_t = 4.5 (cutoff dominated), γ_fit 1.50 vs pred 1.15: clearly non-critical.

**Retrieved information:**
- Beggs & Plenz 2003 [E2] (https://www.jneurosci.org/content/jneuro/23/35/11167.full.pdf): lifetime "revealed an initial slope close to 2" (cultures, Fig. 5; acute slices, Fig. 6E inset), and "the slopes 3/2 for avalanche sizes and 2 for avalanche life times have been predicted by theory for a critical branching process (Harris, 1989; Zapperi et al., 1995)". Lifetimes are counted in frames of width Δt and shown collapsed as t/Δt; the paper gives no fit range or numeric lifetime exponent with an error. Size exponent "−1.50 ± 0.008"; acute slices "1.58 ± 0.04 for electrode data (r 0.999; 1–8 electrodes)".
- Fontenele et al. 2019, PRL 122:208101 [E2, read via the abstract and quoted text in search results, not the PDF] (https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.122.208101): at the critical CV, "τ = 1.52 ± 0.09 and τ_t = 1.7 ± 0.1 for the anesthetized rats", and τ_t's disagreement with MF-DP is cited as pointing to a different universality class. They also state that the ⟨S⟩∼T^{1/σνz} scaling alone "is known to hold even far from criticality", so they use equality of both sides of the crackling relation as the stricter test.
- Carvalho et al. 2021, Front. Neural Circuits [E2, read from quoted text] (https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/neural-circuits/articles/10.3389/fncir.2020.576727/full): subsampled MF-DP models give apparent τ*=1.65 ± 0.02, τ_t*=1.87 ± 0.03, 1/(σνz)*=1.34 ± 0.02; "subsampling the model and adjusting the time bin ... are sufficient ingredients to change the apparent exponents"; full sampling recovers 3/2, 2, 2.
- Destexhe & Touboul 2021, eNeuro [E2] (https://www.eneuro.org/content/8/2/ENEURO.0551-20.2021): their non-critical Brunel and surrogate models, fit with power laws truncated to a cutoff as in Fontenele, satisfy the crackling relation for a connected region of threshold choices.
- Physical Review Research 6:023131 (2024), "Topology-dependent coalescence controls scaling exponents in finite networks" [E2, quoted text only] (https://doi.org/10.1103/physrevresearch.6.023131): "the crackling noise relation is fulfilled even for the quasicritical networks".

**Mathematical derivations:**
- Lifetime law: extinction by generation t means every lineage dies by then, so q_{t+1} = f(q_t) with the Poisson pgf f(s) = e^{σ(s−1)}. At σ=1, expanding f near s=1 (variance 1) gives 1−q_t ≈ 2/t asymptotically (standard; Kolmogorov's estimate). The exact iterate shows t·(1−q_t) = 1.58 at t=10, so the approach to the t^{−2} density is slow; at t≈10 the effective exponent is ≈1.72, not 2.
- For σ<1, 1−q_t decays like c·σ^t, so the local slope goes to −∞ on a scale t ~ 1/(1−σ) (20 generations at σ=0.95). Below that scale the σ<1 and σ=1 laws differ only by the factor σ^t ≈ 1 − (1−σ)t, which on T ≤ 12 changes the OLS slope by ≈0.02–0.03 (computed above: −1.585 vs −1.563).

**Logical deductions:**
- On S ≤ 60 and T ≤ 12 the three diagnostics (size slope, lifetime slope, γ from ⟨S|T⟩) differ between σ=1 and σ=0.95 by ≤0.03, 0.03 and 0.005. A test that cannot separate these values cannot certify σ=1 over σ=0.95 at that window.
- In this model, "crackling relation holds on the fitted window" is neither necessary for criticality (σ=1 fails it by 0.38–0.48 on short windows) nor sufficient on this evidence (not tested here; the 2021/2024 papers report non-critical models passing it).

**Empirical findings:**
- (E1, high) For an exactly critical, fully sampled, unbinned branching process, short-window fits give τ_t ≈ 1.5–1.66 and γ ≈ 1.5–1.7, not 2 and 2, and the crackling relation fails by ~0.4–0.5. Exact recursion and simulation agree.
- (E1, high) On those same windows, σ=0.95 and σ=0.90 give nearly identical lifetime slopes and γ. The diagnostics separate only on windows past ~1/(1−σ) generations (validated at T 50–1000).

**Inferences:**
- (moderate) Beggs & Plenz's lifetime "initial slope close to 2" is not what an ideal critical GW gives over its first few generations (exact: −1.40 to −1.56 over t=3–5). So either their frame-lifetime does not map one to one onto generations (likely: binning merges and splits generations, and multiple electrodes activate per frame), or the "close to 2" is an eyeball reading of the Fig. 5 line. This is a gap in the theory-to-data link, not evidence against criticality. I can't tell which without their lifetime histograms.
- (low–moderate) Part of the departure from MF-DP of Fontenele's τ_t = 1.7 ± 0.1 could be short-window correction to scaling, beside the subsampling and binning effects Carvalho et al. showed. My S 5–200 / T 3–30 MLE on a full-sampled critical GW gives τ=1.50, τ_t=1.66, close to their values. But I do not know their fit ranges or their time-bin-to-generation mapping, so this is a hypothesis, not a finding.
- (moderate) The crackling test separates critical from slightly subcritical branching only when durations well beyond the subcritical cutoff are observable. That is exactly the regime that small arrays and short recordings lack.

**Hypotheses:**
- H11.5: refitting Fontenele's protocol (power law truncated to a cutoff, AIC vs lognormal, their duration range in bins) on full-sampled critical GW reproduces τ_t ≈ 1.7 without any subsampling. Test: read their Supplement S14 for fit ranges and apply the same ranges to the `crackling.py` data, with a bin-merging step.
- H11.6: with Beggs-style frame binning (Δt merging k generations), the lifetime initial slope of critical GW steepens toward −2. Test: bin generations into frames of k=1,2,3 and recompute.

**Theoretical explanations:**
- Correction to scaling: at σ=1 the survival 1−q_t approaches 2/t only slowly from below (1.58/t at t=10), so the density's effective exponent climbs from ~1.4 to 2 over two decades of t. Short windows therefore see shallower lifetime exponents. Both γ estimates come out below 2, but γ_pred is biased much lower (≈1.05 vs ≈1.53 at T 2–12), because τ_t−1 ≈ 0.5 instead of 1 while τ−1 stays ≈ 0.5.

**Speculation:**
- Speculative: the "linear spread" of experimental (τ, τ_t) pairs Fontenele reports might be partly traceable to where each study's fit window starts, if windows sit inside this correction region. Unchecked.

**Unknowns:**
- Fontenele's actual fit ranges and the generation-to-bin mapping (their SI) would settle H11.5.
- Beggs' lifetime histograms or fit range (no numeric lifetime exponent in the 2003 text) would settle whether "close to 2" survives a fit.
- Whether these conclusions hold for non-Poisson offspring (binary, negative binomial) and for spatially embedded networks with refractoriness. Not tested.
- I read Carvalho 2021, Destexhe–Touboul 2021, and PRR 2024 only through quoted passages, not in full.

**Contradictions:**
- Possible tension with Carvalho 2021's "When we sampled from the whole network, we recovered the true critical exponents": my full-sampled σ=1 gives τ_t ≈ 1.5–1.66 on short windows. Likely not a real contradiction. Their windows presumably sat in the scaling regime, and my T 50–1000 window recovers 1.965. Not verified, since I didn't read their fit ranges.
- No contradiction with E048. This extends it: the size slope, the lifetime slope, and γ all fail to separate σ=0.95 from σ=1 on short windows.

**Sources:**
1. Beggs JM, Plenz D (2003) J Neurosci 23:11167. https://doi.org/10.1523/JNEUROSCI.23-35-11167.2003
2. Fontenele AJ et al. (2019) PRL 122:208101. https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.122.208101
3. Carvalho TTA et al. (2021) Front Neural Circuits 14:576727. https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/neural-circuits/articles/10.3389/fncir.2020.576727/full
4. Destexhe A, Touboul JD (2021) eNeuro 8:ENEURO.0551-20.2021. https://www.eneuro.org/content/8/2/ENEURO.0551-20.2021
5. Phys Rev Research 6:023131 (2024). https://doi.org/10.1103/physrevresearch.6.023131
6. Scripts: `notes/cycle16/crackling.py`, `exact_lifetime.py`, `asymptote_check.py`, plus `.json` and `.out` outputs.

**Corrections to earlier entries:**
- Clarification to E048's derivation note "critical GW ... P(s)∼s^{−3/2}; lifetime −2": these are asymptotic exponents. For lifetime, the approach is slow enough that a short window gives ≈ −1.5. E048's own numbers are unaffected.

**Thread update:** T11 active · last touched 2026-11-09 (E054) · H11.2 and H11.4 answered within the model. Next (unordered): H11.5 (Fontenele fit ranges on a full-sampled GW), H11.6 (frame binning vs Beggs' lifetime −2), H11.1 (σ̂ estimator bias), H11.3 (Clauset on capped samples).

1. "Short-window τ_t, γ and crackling read the same for σ=1 and 0.95." (b) Appropriately supported: two routes (exact recursion and simulation) agree for lifetime, the simulation covers γ, and the code was validated on asymptotic windows.
2. "Critical GW fails the crackling relation on short windows by ~0.4–0.5." (b) within this model and these windows. The number depends on window choice (0.48 vs 0.38), which the entry reports instead of picking one.
3. Inference about Beggs' "close to 2": (b) as stated, framed as a gap in the link with two candidate explanations. (e) for deciding between them.
4. Fontenele τ_t inference: (e). Labelled low–moderate and turned into H11.5, which is the right level. I compared my window choice with theirs without knowing their range, so the closeness (1.66 vs 1.7) is suggestive at most.
5. Failure modes that apply: the literature numbers from Fontenele and Carvalho were retrieved from quoted text and not verified against the PDFs (flagged in Unknowns). No non-detection treated as absence. Statistical SEs are tiny, but the window effects (systematic) dominate; the entry keeps the two apart.
